--- Commit Message ---
LU-19916 mgs: start MGS follower on MDTs/OSTs

Start an MGS follower on one MDT or OST of each server node, to serve
the configuration it has a copy of - the <fsname>-client, params and
sptlrpc logs - to the clients attached to it; imperative recovery is
wired up in the next patch.  MGS0 is the leader; MDTs and OSTs still
connect to it, and a follower rejects them with -ENODEV.  MGS0, on its
own MGT or combined with MDT0, takes over from a follower another
target on the host already started.  The follower is optional: an MDT
or OST mounts without it.  Once the MGS is stopped the MGC requeue
thread starts a follower on another target if it can.

IS_MGS0() is what now means "holds the MGT".  Which target runs the
follower is kept by its MGS mount registration, server_mgs_runs_on(),
rather than in lsi_flags, which other threads update without a lock.

A follower is a readonly backup of MGS0: no ioctls, and no nodemap
config file - it refuses a nodemap config read rather than serving one
it does not have.  Replicating the nodemap configuration, so that it
too survives the loss of MGS0, is left to a later patch, and so is
replicating _mgs-sptlrpc.  A follower refuses a client that connects
with an sptlrpc flavor, which MGS0 then serves instead.

A follower writes no config log into CONFIGS/, which on a follower
belongs to the MDT or OST it runs on and holds that target's local
copy of the logs, and it builds its fsdb without reading
<fsname>-client, since it edits no config.  For the same reason it
leaves that target's lu_site cache alone, and only the leader sends
LCFG_PRE_CLEANUP, which would stop the OI scrub of a target that is
still serving.

MGS followers are off by default: an MDT or OST starts one only if
mounted with the new "mgs_follower" option, and
LUSTRE_MGS_FOLLOWER_DEFAULT turns them on everywhere at compile time.
test-framework mounts every MDT and OST with "mgs_follower", unless
MGS_FOLLOWER=false, so the feature is still tested.  "nomgs", "nosvc"
and a read-only mount all keep the follower off a target; the last so
that an lsnapshot mount does not serve the snapshot's configuration.

That target is already serving and the site is shared, so
mgs_fs_setup() no longer publishes itself as ls_top_dev and clears it
afterwards: that left the live MDT/OST with a NULL top device,
crashing the next mdt_object_find() or ofd_object_find().  Nothing
there needs it - local storage resolves through ls_locate() and the
root through dt_locate_at() - which is also why mgc_fs_setup() does
the same work without it.  For the same reason mgs_fs_setup() and
nidtbl_read_version() resolve their objects through the local storage
device rather than through the MGS layer, which would leave an
MGS-layer object pinned in a site only that target can purge.

Leaving ls_top_dev alone also fixes the index backup at umount of a
standalone MGT: lustre_index_backup() skips it when ls_top_dev is
NULL, which is how mgs_fs_setup() has always left it.

Telling a follower about config changes, so that it can revoke its
clients' config locks, and applying the mgs.srpc.flavor.* rules of
MGS0 are left to the next patch; until then a follower's clients see
config changes only when they reconnect.

OBD_CONNECT_FTM, set only by a client mount, is what a follower
accepts a connection on.  A target that reuses the MGC a client mount
started drops the flag and reconnects, so no follower serves it.  The
follow-on patch gives the server MGC its own device name, so a client
and a target on one node never share a connection's flags.

server_mgs_lock() serializes starting and stopping the MGS between
mounts and the MGC requeue thread.  It nests outside cld_lock, which
cld_start_mgs() takes under it.  server_fill_super() does not hold
it across lustre_start_mgc(): the cleanup of the node's last MGC
waits for the requeue thread, which can be waiting for that lock in
cld_start_mgs().

Add parameter mgs.MGS.state, "leader" or "follower", which is_mgs()
in liblustreapi checks because a follower handles no ioctl.

conf-sanity test_5b starts its OST with "nomgs": it checks that a
client cannot mount with no MGS, and an OST that starts a follower
here has no MGS0 to feed it, so the OST's own MGC is refused by that
follower for as long as the test runs.  replay-single test_61d drops
CFS_FAIL_ONCE from its llog_setup failure, which a follower started
on another target of that node would otherwise spend before the mount
under test reaches it.

Test-Parameters: mdtcount=4 testlist=conf-sanity serverversion=2.17 env=CONF_SANITY_EXCEPT="73e 88a 250"
Test-Parameters: mdtcount=4 testlist=conf-sanity clientversion=2.17 env=CONF_SANITY_EXCEPT=104a
Test-Parameters: mdtcount=4 testlist=replay-single env=ONLY=61,ONLY_REPEAT=20

Patch Set 30:

(15 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some significant concerns, see inline comments. Two of the three headline behaviours described in the commit message do not appear to work as written: no MGC ever sets OBD_CONNECT_FTM, so a follower rejects every connection including clients, and the log-name match in cld_should_start_mgs() is lower-case while target config log names are upper-case, so a follower is never restarted on another target. This is a large piece of new user-visible functionality (MGS followers on every target, a new mgs.MGS.state parameter, a new connect-time rejection) with no test exercising it - the only conf-sanity change disables an existing test. Could conf-sanity gain a case that mounts an MDT/OST, checks a follower came up, mounts a client against it, and then unmounts the MGS0 to verify the follower migrates?

Patch Set 33:

(4 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The patch adds a lot of new user-visible behaviour - an MGS device now appears on every MDT/OST, `mgs.MGS.state` is new, and "nomgs"/"nosvc"/"rdonly_dev" now gate it - but no test exercises any of it. Should conf-sanity get a case that mounts a plain OST, checks `mgs.MGS.state` is "follower", verifies the MGS0 takeover and the requeue-thread restart after MGS0 stops, and confirms "-o nomgs" keeps the follower off? Also worth checking that suites which key off `lctl dl` output or `do_facet mgs` still behave now that every server node has an "MGS" device.
